      README that gets you interviews

      One-line pitch, live link, GIF, features, tech stack with why for each choice, architecture diagram (providers → routes → features), testing approach (unit, component, E2E, a11y), performance numbers from M9, and "what I'd do next". See What every showcase project needs.

      Interview questions this milestone prepares

      Unit vs integration vs E2E: which did you write and why?

      Unit tests for the reducer and stats (pure logic), component tests with Testing Library and MSW for user flows and data states, and a few Playwright tests for the critical journeys and accessibility. Most confidence per minute comes from component tests.

      Why does refreshing a deep link 404 on a static host, and how do you fix it?

      The server looks for a file at that path. Rewrite all paths to index.html so the client-side router handles them.

      What runs in your CI pipeline?

      Install from the lock file, lint, type-check, unit/component tests with coverage, production build, E2E in a headless browser. Pull requests can't merge if it fails.

      Done when

      The app is live, CI is green with a badge in the README, E2E and axe pass, and you've posted it on LinkedIn. Phase 1 complete: this project alone covers most of a React interview.

      M11 · Phase 2: Next.js, Supabase and an AI feature

      Turn JobPilot into a full-stack product: accounts, a real database, server rendering, and an AI assistant that reads job descriptions. This phase covers the Next.js, SQL, security and AI parts of the handbook.

      Build

      1. Move to Next.js (App Router, TypeScript). Reuse your components, reducer, schemas and tests; pages become route folders.
      2. Supabase: Postgres database, email/Google sign-in, row-level security.
      3. Server-first data: server components read applications; server actions create/update/delete them.
      4. AI job-description parser: paste a job description → AI returns structured fields (company, role, location, skills, salary if present) that pre-fill the form.
      5. AI tailored bullets (stretch): stream 3 resume bullets tailored to the job, based on your saved profile.

      Concepts: why and syntax

      Schema and row-level security (Schema design, SQL in app code)

      sql
      create table applications (
  id          uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
  user_id     uuid not null references auth.users(id) on delete cascade,
  company     text not null,
  role        text not null,
  status      text not null default 'wishlist'
              check (status in ('wishlist','applied','interview','offer','rejected')),
  url         text,
  location    text,
  notes       text,
  tags        text[] not null default '{}',
  follow_up_on date,
  created_at  timestamptz not null default now(),
  updated_at  timestamptz not null default now()
);
create index applications_user_updated on applications (user_id, updated_at desc);

alter table applications enable row level security;             -- without this, anyone can read everything
create policy "users manage their own applications" on applications
  for all using (user_id = auth.uid()) with check (user_id = auth.uid());

      Practise SQL on your own data: response rate per month with GROUP BY date_trunc('month', created_at), time from applied to first interview, companies you applied to twice.

      Server components and server actions (Server vs Client Components, Server Actions)

      tsx
      // app/(app)/board/page.tsx — a server component: data loads before HTML is sent
export default async function BoardPage() {
  const supabase = await createServerClient();                  // reads the session cookie
  const { data: apps, error } = await supabase
    .from("applications").select("*").order("updated_at", { ascending: false });
  if (error) throw error;                                        // caught by error.tsx
  return <Board initialApps={apps} />;                           // client component for drag & drop
}
      ts
      // app/(app)/board/actions.ts
"use server";
const MoveSchema = z.object({ id: z.uuid(), status: z.enum(STATUSES) });

export async function moveApplication(input: unknown) {
  const { id, status } = MoveSchema.parse(input);               // never trust the client
  const supabase = await createServerClient();
  const { data: { user } } = await supabase.auth.getUser();
  if (!user) throw new Error("Not signed in");
  const { error } = await supabase.from("applications")
    .update({ status, updated_at: new Date().toISOString() }).eq("id", id);   // RLS limits it to the user's rows
  if (error) throw new Error("Could not move the application");
  revalidatePath("/board");
}

      On the client, wrap the call with useOptimistic so the card moves instantly and rolls back if the action fails (React 19 hooks).

      The AI feature (Building AI features)

      The pattern, independent of the provider you choose:

      1. A server-only route handler or server action receives the pasted text. The API key lives in a server environment variable, never in the browser.
      2. Check the user is signed in, limit input length, and rate-limit per user.
      3. Ask the model for structured output matching a Zod schema (the Vercel AI SDK supports this directly), so you get { company, role, location, skills[] }, not free text.
      4. Validate the result with the same Zod schema, then pre-fill the form so the user reviews it before saving. The AI suggests; the user decides.
      5. For tailored bullets, stream the response token by token with a Stop button, and render it as plain text or sanitised Markdown.
      ts
      const ParsedJob = z.object({
  company: z.string(),
  role: z.string(),
  location: z.string().optional(),
  skills: z.array(z.string()).max(15),
  salaryRange: z.string().optional(),
});

      AI SDKs change quickly: follow the current docs for the exact function names (structured output and streaming), and keep your Zod schema as the contract.

      In Vue you'd write

      Next.jsNuxt
      async server componentuseFetch / useAsyncData in a page
      server actionserver/api/*.post.ts + $fetch
      route handler for AI streamingNitro server route returning a stream
      proxy.ts for auth redirectsroute middleware

      Bonus: since ShellScholars is Nuxt + Supabase, you can apply the same schema, RLS and AI patterns there, showing both frameworks on your resume.

      Tests

      • Unit-test the Zod schemas and any pure server logic (input validation rejects bad data).
      • Keep the component tests from phase 1; mock server actions with vi.mock.
      • Playwright: sign up with a test account, add and move an application, sign out, confirm another test user can't see it (proves RLS).
      • Mock the AI endpoint in E2E with page.route() so tests are fast, free and deterministic.

      Interview questions this milestone prepares

      Server components vs client components: how did you split JobPilot?

      Pages fetch data on the server (no loading spinners, no API keys in the browser); the board is a client component because it needs drag and drop and optimistic state.

      How do you make sure users only see their own data?

      Row-level security in Postgres (user_id = auth.uid()), plus authentication checks and input validation in every server action. The UI hiding things is not security.

      How did you integrate AI safely?

      Server-side calls only, auth and rate limits, input length limits, structured output validated with Zod, user review before saving, streamed output rendered safely, and awareness of prompt injection from pasted text.

      Why did you migrate from Vite SPA to Next.js?

      To add accounts and a database with server-side data access, keep secrets on the server, and render faster first loads. The SPA was right for a local-only tool; the product needs changed.

      Done when

      Multiple users can sign up and use JobPilot safely, the AI parser works on real job descriptions, CI and E2E still pass, and the README explains the architecture and the AI design.

      Part

      Project 2: SyncBoard, a local-first collaborative board

      A Trello-style board that works fully offline, syncs in real time between users, merges conflicting edits automatically, shows who's online and where their cursor is, supports undo/redo, permissions and installation as an app, and is tested with two browsers editing at once. Built with React, TypeScript, Yjs, Hocuspocus, IndexedDB and dnd-kit.

      What you'll build, architecture and tech choices

      Local-first means the data lives on the user's device first. Every action writes to a local copy instantly (so the UI never waits for a server), the copy is saved to IndexedDB, and a sync layer exchanges changes with other devices whenever a connection exists. A CRDT makes sure all copies end up identical, however edits interleave.

      Features, and what each one proves

      FeatureProves
      Boards with columns and cards; drag and drop with mouse, touch and keyboardComponent architecture, accessible drag and drop, ordering algorithms
      Works with no network; reload offline still shows your dataIndexedDB, local-first data flow, service workers
      Real-time sync between users and devicesWebSockets, CRDTs, sync protocols
      Concurrent edits never lose data or divergeConflict resolution, data-integrity repair, deterministic ordering
      Live cursors, avatars, "Priya is editing"Ephemeral presence state, throttling
      Collaborative rich-text card descriptionsEditor integration with CRDT text
      Undo/redo that never undoes other people's workTransaction origins, command history
      Owner / editor / viewer roles, invite linksAuth for WebSockets, SQL schema, server-side enforcement
      Installable app that loads offline and updates safelyPWA manifest, service worker lifecycle
      E2E tests with two browsers, including offlineTesting real-time systems deterministically

      Architecture

      text
       Browser A (React)                                  Browser B (React)
 ┌───────────────────────────────┐                 ┌───────────────────────────────┐
 │ UI ──reads──▶ useBoard() snapshot                │ same                          │
 │ UI ──actions─▶ board ops ──▶ Y.Doc (in memory)   │                               │
 │                     │   ▲                        │                               │
 │   y-indexeddb ◀─────┘   │ updates                │                               │
 │   (IndexedDB on disk)   │                        │                               │
 │   HocuspocusProvider ◀──┘──── WebSocket (wss) ───┼──▶ Sync server (Hocuspocus)   │
 │   awareness (cursors)                          ◀─┼─── broadcasts updates to peers│
 └───────────────────────────────┘                 └───────────────────────────────┘
                                   Sync server
                                   ├─ onAuthenticate: verify token, check role (SQL)
                                   ├─ persistence: Yjs updates in SQLite (binary)
                                   └─ REST API (Hono): login, boards, members, invites

      Why these technologies

      ChoiceWhyAlternatives and trade-offs
      Yjs (CRDT)Mature, very fast, huge editor ecosystem, works peer-to-peer or through any server, you control your dataAutomerge: JSON-like documents, great history, Rust core. Liveblocks: hosted, fastest to ship, less to learn. Zero / ElectricSQL: sync engines over Postgres with server authority, better for relational queries, different model
      Hocuspocus (sync server)Production-grade Yjs WebSocket server with authentication hooks, read-only connections, persistence and scaling extensionsy-websocket server: minimal, fine for learning, you add auth/persistence yourself
      y-indexeddbStores the document locally so it loads instantly and offlineOPFS/SQLite-wasm for very large data
      fractional-indexingCard order as sortable strings, so concurrent moves don't clash (explained in C1)Y.Array with move = delete + insert, which duplicates cards under concurrent moves
      dnd-kitAccessible drag and drop with keyboard and screen-reader supportNative HTML drag and drop (no touch or keyboard), pragmatic-drag-and-drop
      Hono + SQLiteSmall, typed REST API for accounts, boards and roles; real SQL practiceSupabase/Postgres for production auth and scale

      Using Vue instead? The architecture is identical: board ops, Yjs, IndexedDB, Hocuspocus and the server don't change. Replace the useSyncExternalStore binding with a shallowRef refreshed on doc.on("update"), and use a Vue drag-and-drop library (VueUse's useSortable or vue-draggable-plus). Doing the web app in React is still recommended, because most job posts ask for React.

      See Monorepos. Exporting the TypeScript source directly works because both Vite and tsx compile it on the fly.

      Concepts: why and syntax

      The data model: a Y.Doc of nested Y.Maps

      Why Y.Map per card instead of one plain object: each field (title, column, order) becomes independently mergeable. If Alice renames a card while Bob moves it, both changes survive. With one plain object, one write would overwrite the other.

      ts
      // packages/shared/src/board.ts
import * as Y from "yjs";
import { generateKeyBetween } from "fractional-indexing";

export const LOCAL_ORIGIN = "local-user";           // tags this user's transactions (undo, C8)

export type ColumnData = { id: string; title: string; order: string };
export type CardData = {
  id: string; title: string; columnId: string; order: string;
  createdBy: string; updatedAt: number;
};

export function getBoard(doc: Y.Doc) {
  return {
    meta: doc.getMap("meta"),                        // schemaVersion, title
    columns: doc.getMap<Y.Map<unknown>>("columns"),  // columnId → Y.Map
    cards: doc.getMap<Y.Map<unknown>>("cards"),      // cardId → Y.Map (flat, not nested in columns)
  };
}

      Why cards are a flat map with a columnId field, not arrays inside columns: moving a card becomes "set one field", which merges cleanly. Moving an item between arrays is a delete + insert, and two people moving the same card concurrently would produce two copies.

      Ordering with fractional indexes

      Each card has an order string like "a0", "a0V", "a1". To put a card between two others, generate a key that sorts between their keys. No other card changes, so concurrent reorders don't conflict.

      ts
      const sortByOrder = <T extends { order: string; id: string }>(a: T, b: T) =>
  a.order < b.order ? -1 : a.order > b.order ? 1 : a.id < b.id ? -1 : 1;   // tie → by id, deterministic
// Compare with < and >, NOT localeCompare: keys are ASCII base-62 and must sort by character code.

export function readColumns(doc: Y.Doc): ColumnData[] {
  return [...getBoard(doc).columns.values()].map(c => c.toJSON() as ColumnData).sort(sortByOrder);
}
export function readCards(doc: Y.Doc, columnId: string): CardData[] {
  return [...getBoard(doc).cards.values()]
    .map(c => c.toJSON() as CardData)
    .filter(c => c.columnId === columnId)
    .sort(sortByOrder);
}

      Board operations inside transactions

      Why transactions: several field changes become one update (one network message, one undo step, one re-render), and the origin tag lets the undo manager tell your changes from everyone else's.

      ts
      export function addColumn(doc: Y.Doc, title: string, origin: unknown = LOCAL_ORIGIN): string {
  const id = crypto.randomUUID();
  const last = readColumns(doc).at(-1)?.order ?? null;
  doc.transact(() => {
    const col = new Y.Map<unknown>();
    col.set("id", id);
    col.set("title", title);
    col.set("order", generateKeyBetween(last, null));
    getBoard(doc).columns.set(id, col);
  }, origin);
  return id;
}

export function addCard(doc: Y.Doc, input: { columnId: string; title: string; userId: string },
                        origin: unknown = LOCAL_ORIGIN): string {
  const id = crypto.randomUUID();
  const last = readCards(doc, input.columnId).at(-1)?.order ?? null;
  doc.transact(() => {
    const card = new Y.Map<unknown>();
    card.set("id", id);
    card.set("title", input.title.trim());
    card.set("columnId", input.columnId);
    card.set("order", generateKeyBetween(last, null));
    card.set("description", new Y.XmlFragment());       // rich text, used in C7
    card.set("createdBy", input.userId);
    card.set("updatedAt", Date.now());
    getBoard(doc).cards.set(id, card);
  }, origin);
  return id;
}

export function renameCard(doc: Y.Doc, id: string, title: string, origin: unknown = LOCAL_ORIGIN): boolean {
  const card = getBoard(doc).cards.get(id);
  if (!card) return false;                               // deleted by someone else meanwhile
  doc.transact(() => { card.set("title", title.trim()); card.set("updatedAt", Date.now()); }, origin);
  return true;
}

export function deleteColumn(doc: Y.Doc, columnId: string, origin: unknown = LOCAL_ORIGIN) {
  const { columns, cards } = getBoard(doc);
  doc.transact(() => {
    for (const [id, card] of cards) if (card.get("columnId") === columnId) cards.delete(id);
    columns.delete(columnId);
  }, origin);
}

      Binding Yjs to React with useSyncExternalStore

      Why this hook: the Y.Doc is an external store that changes outside React (remote updates arrive over the network). useSyncExternalStore is React's official way to subscribe to external stores without tearing (different components seeing different versions in one render).

      ts
      // apps/web/src/sync/useBoard.ts
export type BoardSnapshot = { columns: ColumnData[]; cardsByColumn: Record<string, CardData[]> };

function readBoard(doc: Y.Doc): BoardSnapshot {
  const columns = readColumns(doc);
  return { columns, cardsByColumn: Object.fromEntries(columns.map(c => [c.id, readCards(doc, c.id)])) };
}

function createBoardStore(doc: Y.Doc) {
  let snapshot = readBoard(doc);                        // cached: getSnapshot must return the same object until data changes
  return {
    subscribe(onStoreChange: () => void) {
      const handler = () => { snapshot = readBoard(doc); onStoreChange(); };
      doc.on("update", handler);                        // fires for local AND remote changes
      return () => doc.off("update", handler);
    },
    getSnapshot: () => snapshot,
  };
}

export function useBoard(doc: Y.Doc): BoardSnapshot {
  const store = useMemo(() => createBoardStore(doc), [doc]);
  return useSyncExternalStore(store.subscribe, store.getSnapshot);
}

      Re-reading the whole board on each update is fine for hundreds of cards. At thousands, subscribe per column or per card with observeDeep and cache unchanged objects so memo can skip them.

      Tests

      ts
      // packages/shared/src/board.test.ts
it("adds cards in order and reads them sorted", () => {
  const doc = new Y.Doc();
  const col = addColumn(doc, "To do");
  addCard(doc, { columnId: col, title: "First", userId: "u1" });
  addCard(doc, { columnId: col, title: "Second", userId: "u1" });
  expect(readCards(doc, col).map(c => c.title)).toEqual(["First", "Second"]);
});
it("deleting a column deletes its cards in one transaction", () => {
  const doc = new Y.Doc();
  const col = addColumn(doc, "To do");
  addCard(doc, { columnId: col, title: "A", userId: "u1" });
  let updates = 0;
  doc.on("update", () => updates++);
  deleteColumn(doc, col);
  expect(getBoard(doc).cards.size).toBe(0);
  expect(updates).toBe(1);                               // one transaction = one update
});
it("renaming a deleted card is a safe no-op", () => {
  expect(renameCard(new Y.Doc(), "missing", "x")).toBe(false);
});

      Interview questions this milestone prepares

      What is a CRDT, in one minute?

      A Conflict-free Replicated Data Type: a data structure that every replica can edit independently, and whose merge is designed so all replicas converge to the same state regardless of the order updates arrive, without a central server deciding.

      Why store card order as fractional index strings instead of array positions?

      Moving a card changes only that card's key, so concurrent moves of different cards never conflict, and a concurrent move of the same card resolves to one position instead of duplicating it (array move = delete + insert).

      What does useSyncExternalStore solve?

      Subscribing React to state that lives outside React, safely under concurrent rendering (no tearing), with a cached snapshot so unchanged data doesn't re-render.

      Done when

      You can create columns and cards in a single browser, the UI updates through useBoard, and the shared package has passing unit tests.

      C2 · Accessible drag and drop with fractional ordering

      Build

      • Reorder cards within a column and move them between columns by mouse, touch and keyboard (Space to pick up, arrows to move, Space to drop, Escape to cancel).
      • Screen-reader announcements ("Picked up card Write tests. Moved to Done, position 2 of 4").
      • A drag overlay so the card follows the pointer smoothly.

      Concepts: why and syntax

      Moving a card = two field writes

      ts
      // packages/shared/src/board.ts
export function moveCard(doc: Y.Doc, cardId: string, toColumnId: string, index: number,
                         origin: unknown = LOCAL_ORIGIN): boolean {
  const { cards, columns } = getBoard(doc);
  const card = cards.get(cardId);
  if (!card || !columns.has(toColumnId)) return false;       // deleted remotely: ignore safely

  const siblings = readCards(doc, toColumnId).filter(c => c.id !== cardId);
  index = Math.min(Math.max(index, 0), siblings.length);    // clamp: never trust callers blindly
  const before = siblings[index - 1]?.order ?? null;
  let after = siblings[index]?.order ?? null;
  if (before !== null && after !== null && before >= after) after = null;  // equal keys from concurrent inserts (C5 repairs them)

  doc.transact(() => {
    card.set("columnId", toColumnId);
    card.set("order", generateKeyBetween(before, after));
    card.set("updatedAt", Date.now());
  }, origin);
  return true;
}

      dnd-kit: sensors, sortable columns, drop handling

      bash
      pnpm --filter web add @dnd-kit/core @dnd-kit/sortable @dnd-kit/utilities
      tsx
      // apps/web/src/board/Board.tsx
const sensors = useSensors(
  useSensor(PointerSensor, { activationConstraint: { distance: 5 } }),        // small move before drag: clicks still work
  useSensor(TouchSensor, { activationConstraint: { delay: 150, tolerance: 5 } }),  // long-press on phones, scrolling still works
  useSensor(KeyboardSensor, { coordinateGetter: sortableKeyboardCoordinates }),
);

function handleDragEnd({ active, over }: DragEndEvent) {
  setActiveId(null);
  if (!over) return;
  const cardId = String(active.id);
  const overId = String(over.id);
  // "over" is either a column (dropped on empty space) or another card
  const toColumnId = board.cardsByColumn[overId]
    ? overId
    : board.columns.find(col => board.cardsByColumn[col.id].some(c => c.id === overId))!.id;
  const overIndex = board.cardsByColumn[toColumnId].findIndex(c => c.id === overId);
  const index = overIndex === -1 ? board.cardsByColumn[toColumnId].filter(c => c.id !== cardId).length : overIndex;
  moveCard(doc, cardId, toColumnId, index);
}

return (
  <DndContext sensors={sensors} collisionDetection={closestCorners}
              onDragStart={e => setActiveId(String(e.active.id))} onDragEnd={handleDragEnd}
              onDragCancel={() => setActiveId(null)} accessibility={{ announcements }}>
    <div className="board">
      {board.columns.map(col => <Column key={col.id} column={col} cards={board.cardsByColumn[col.id]} />)}
    </div>
    <DragOverlay>{activeCard ? <CardView card={activeCard} dragging /> : null}</DragOverlay>
  </DndContext>
);

      Why index = overIndex works in every case: moving down within a column, the dragged card is removed from the sibling list, so inserting at the old index of the target lands just after it; moving up or across columns lands just before it. Walk through both cases on paper once.

      tsx
      // Column.tsx
function Column({ column, cards }: { column: ColumnData; cards: CardData[] }) {
  const { setNodeRef } = useDroppable({ id: column.id });
  return (
    <section aria-labelledby={`col-${column.id}`} className="column">
      <h2 id={`col-${column.id}`}>{column.title} <span>({cards.length})</span></h2>
      <SortableContext items={cards.map(c => c.id)} strategy={verticalListSortingStrategy}>
        <ul ref={setNodeRef}>{cards.map(c => <SortableCard key={c.id} card={c} />)}</ul>
      </SortableContext>
    </section>
  );
}
// SortableCard.tsx
function SortableCard({ card }: { card: CardData }) {
  const { attributes, listeners, setNodeRef, transform, transition, isDragging } = useSortable({ id: card.id });
  return (
    <li ref={setNodeRef} {...attributes} {...listeners}
        style={{ transform: CSS.Transform.toString(transform), transition, opacity: isDragging ? 0.4 : 1 }}>
      <CardView card={card} />
    </li>
  );
}
      ts
      // Screen-reader announcements: say what happened in human words
const announcements: Announcements = {
  onDragStart: ({ active }) => `Picked up card ${titleOf(active.id)}.`,
  onDragOver: ({ active, over }) => over ? `Card ${titleOf(active.id)} is over ${describe(over.id)}.` : "",
  onDragEnd: ({ active, over }) => over ? `Card ${titleOf(active.id)} dropped in ${describe(over.id)}.` : "Card returned.",
  onDragCancel: ({ active }) => `Moving cancelled. Card ${titleOf(active.id)} returned to its place.`,
};

      Tests

      Unit-test the ordering logic here; test the actual dragging in Playwright (C11), because drag and drop depends on real layout that jsdom doesn't compute.

      ts
      describe("moveCard", () => {
  function setup() {
    const doc = new Y.Doc();
    const todo = addColumn(doc, "To do"), done = addColumn(doc, "Done");
    const [a, b, c] = ["A", "B", "C"].map(t => addCard(doc, { columnId: todo, title: t, userId: "u" }));
    return { doc, todo, done, a, b, c };
  }
  const titles = (doc: Y.Doc, col: string) => readCards(doc, col).map(x => x.title);

  it("moves a card to the top", () => {
    const { doc, todo, c } = setup();
    moveCard(doc, c, todo, 0);
    expect(titles(doc, todo)).toEqual(["C", "A", "B"]);
  });
  it("moves a card down past its neighbour", () => {
    const { doc, todo, a } = setup();
    moveCard(doc, a, todo, 1);                         // index in the list without A
    expect(titles(doc, todo)).toEqual(["B", "A", "C"]);
  });
  it("moves across columns", () => {
    const { doc, todo, done, b } = setup();
    moveCard(doc, b, done, 0);
    expect(titles(doc, todo)).toEqual(["A", "C"]);
    expect(titles(doc, done)).toEqual(["B"]);
  });
  it("only changes the moved card's fields", () => {
    const { doc, todo, a, c } = setup();
    const before = getBoard(doc).cards.get(a)!.get("order");
    moveCard(doc, c, todo, 0);
    expect(getBoard(doc).cards.get(a)!.get("order")).toBe(before);
  });
});

      Interview questions this milestone prepares

      How do you make drag and drop accessible?

      A keyboard alternative (pick up, move with arrows, drop, cancel), focus management, live-region announcements of what happened, and touch support with long-press so scrolling still works. Native HTML drag and drop provides none of this.

      How does fractional indexing work, and what's its weakness?

      Keys are strings between which you can always generate another key, so inserting between two items changes only the moved item. Concurrent inserts at the same spot can create equal keys; you break ties deterministically (by id) and re-space keys occasionally.

      Done when

      You can reorder and move every card with mouse, touch and keyboard only, announcements read correctly in a screen reader, and moveCard tests pass.

      C3 · Offline-first persistence with IndexedDB

      Build

      • The board loads from the device instantly, before any network, and survives reloads and browser restarts.
      • A "My boards" list that also works offline.
      • Request persistent storage so the browser doesn't evict data under storage pressure.

      Concepts: why and syntax

      Why IndexedDB and not localStorage (Storage)

      localStorageIndexedDB
      APISynchronous (blocks the main thread)Asynchronous
      DataStrings onlyStructured data and binary (Uint8Array): perfect for Yjs updates
      Size~5 MBHundreds of MB or more (a share of disk)
      Transactions and indexesNoYes

      Loading a board locally

      bash
      pnpm --filter web add y-indexeddb idb-keyval
      ts
      // apps/web/src/sync/useLocalBoard.ts
export function useLocalBoard(boardId: string) {
  const [state, setState] = useState<{ doc: Y.Doc; ready: boolean } | null>(null);

  useEffect(() => {
    const doc = new Y.Doc();
    const local = new IndexeddbPersistence(`syncboard:${boardId}`, doc);   // loads saved updates, then saves every new one
    setState({ doc, ready: false });
    local.whenSynced.then(() => setState({ doc, ready: true }));           // local data is in memory now
    return () => { local.destroy(); doc.destroy(); };                       // Strict Mode double-mount safe
  }, [boardId]);

  return state;
}

      Never seed default columns when a board loads. If two devices each find an empty board and each create "To do / Doing / Done", you get six columns after sync. Create defaults exactly once, when a user creates the board, and sync them from there.

      The boards list, cached for offline

      ts
      import { get, set } from "idb-keyval";                  // tiny key-value wrapper over IndexedDB
export async function loadBoards(): Promise<BoardSummary[]> {
  try {
    const fresh = await api.listBoards();               // REST (C9); fails offline
    await set("boards", fresh);
    return fresh;
  } catch {
    return (await get<BoardSummary[]>("boards")) ?? []; // offline: last known list
  }
}

      Protect local data from eviction

      ts
      if (navigator.storage?.persist) {
  const persisted = await navigator.storage.persist();   // browsers may auto-grant for installed/engaged sites
  const { usage, quota } = await navigator.storage.estimate();
  console.info({ persisted, usageMB: (usage ?? 0) / 1e6, quotaMB: (quota ?? 0) / 1e6 });
}

      On sign-out, delete local data (await local.clearData()) so the next person on a shared computer can't see it.

      Tests

      ts
      // vitest setup for the web app
import "fake-indexeddb/auto";                            // an in-memory IndexedDB for Node

it("restores a board after reopening", async () => {
  const doc1 = new Y.Doc();
  const p1 = new IndexeddbPersistence("syncboard:t1", doc1);
  await p1.whenSynced;
  addColumn(doc1, "To do");
  await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 50));             // writes are asynchronous
  await p1.destroy(); doc1.destroy();

  const doc2 = new Y.Doc();
  const p2 = new IndexeddbPersistence("syncboard:t1", doc2);
  await p2.whenSynced;

      Interview questions this milestone prepares

      What does "local-first" mean, and why is it good UX?

      The primary copy of data is on the device: reads and writes are instant and work offline, the network is used for sync and backup. The UI never waits for a server, so it feels fast and reliable on poor connections.

      IndexedDB vs localStorage?

      IndexedDB is asynchronous, transactional, stores structured and binary data, and holds far more. localStorage is synchronous, strings-only and small.

      Can the browser delete IndexedDB data?

      Yes, under storage pressure, unless storage is marked persistent (navigator.storage.persist()). Also Safari may clear script-written storage for sites not visited for a while. That's why syncing to a server matters even in local-first apps.

      Done when

      With the network disabled in DevTools, a reload shows your boards and cards immediately, and you can keep editing.

      C4 · Real-time sync server and connection states

      Build

      • A Hocuspocus sync server that relays updates between clients and stores documents in SQLite.
      • Client connection with a clear status: "All changes synced", "Syncing…", "Offline — saved on this device", "Reconnecting…".
      • Automatic reconnection, plus explicit disconnect/reconnect on the browser's offline/online events.

      Concepts: why and syntax

      How Yjs syncs (the protocol in plain words)

      1. On connect, each side sends its state vector: "for every client I know, here's how many operations I've seen".
      2. Each side replies with only the updates the other is missing (a diff), as compact binary.
      3. After that, every local transaction produces an update that is sent and broadcast to everyone on the same document.
      4. Updates are commutative and idempotent: order of arrival doesn't matter and receiving one twice is harmless. That's what makes offline + reconnect simple.

      The server

      bash
      pnpm --filter server add @hocuspocus/server @hocuspocus/extension-sqlite yjs
pnpm --filter server add -D tsx
# apps/server/package.json → "scripts": { "dev": "tsx watch src/index.ts" }
      ts
      // apps/server/src/sync.ts  (Hocuspocus v3 syntax; v2 uses Server.configure({...}) — check the docs for your version)
import { Server } from "@hocuspocus/server";
import { SQLite } from "@hocuspocus/extension-sqlite";

export const syncServer = new Server({
  port: Number(process.env.SYNC_PORT ?? 1234),
  extensions: [new SQLite({ database: "data/documents.sqlite" })],  // stores each document's Yjs state (binary)
  // onAuthenticate is added in C9
});
syncServer.listen();

      The client provider, layered on top of local persistence

      bash
      pnpm --filter web add @hocuspocus/provider
      ts
      // apps/web/src/sync/useSyncedBoard.ts
type SyncStatus = "connecting" | "connected" | "disconnected";

export function useSyncedBoard(doc: Y.Doc | undefined, boardId: string) {
  const [status, setStatus] = useState<SyncStatus>("connecting");
  const [pending, setPending] = useState(false);
  const [provider, setProvider] = useState<HocuspocusProvider | null>(null);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (!doc) return;
    const p = new HocuspocusProvider({
      url: import.meta.env.VITE_SYNC_URL,              // ws://localhost:1234 in dev, wss://… in production
      name: `board:${boardId}`,
      document: doc,
      token: () => getWsToken(),                       // C9: short-lived token fetched on each (re)connect
      onStatus: ({ status }) => setStatus(status as SyncStatus),
    });
    const onUnsynced = () => setPending(p.hasUnsyncedChanges);   // local edits not yet acknowledged by the server
    p.on("unsyncedChanges", onUnsynced);

    const goOffline = () => p.disconnect();            // don't wait for timeouts when the OS says we're offline
    const goOnline = () => p.connect();
    window.addEventListener("offline", goOffline);
    window.addEventListener("online", goOnline);

    setProvider(p);
    return () => {
      window.removeEventListener("offline", goOffline);
      window.removeEventListener("online", goOnline);
      p.off("unsyncedChanges", onUnsynced);
      p.destroy();
    };
  }, [doc, boardId]);

  return { provider, status, pending };
}
      tsx
      // SyncStatus.tsx — honest, calm wording (see UX writing)
const message =
  status === "disconnected" ? "Offline — your changes are saved on this device and will sync when you reconnect"
  : status === "connecting" ? "Reconnecting…"
  : pending ? "Syncing…"
  : "All changes synced";
<p role="status" aria-live="polite" className={`sync sync-${status}`}>{message}</p>

      Optimistic UI comes for free: every edit is applied to the local document immediately, and sync happens in the background. There's no "saving…" spinner blocking the user.

      Tests

      Test sync logic without a network by exchanging updates between two documents directly (the same bytes the server would relay):

      ts
      // packages/shared/src/testing.ts
export function syncDocs(a: Y.Doc, b: Y.Doc) {
  Y.applyUpdate(b, Y.encodeStateAsUpdate(a, Y.encodeStateVector(b)));   // send a's missing updates to b
  Y.applyUpdate(a, Y.encodeStateAsUpdate(b, Y.encodeStateVector(a)));   // and b's to a
}

it("a card added on one device appears on another", () => {
  const alice = new Y.Doc(), bob = new Y.Doc();
  const col = addColumn(alice, "To do");
  syncDocs(alice, bob);
  addCard(bob, { columnId: col, title: "From Bob", userId: "bob" });
  syncDocs(alice, bob);
  expect(readCards(alice, col).map(c => c.title)).toEqual(["From Bob"]);
});
it("applying the same update twice changes nothing (idempotent)", () => {
  const a = new Y.Doc(); addColumn(a, "X");
  const update = Y.encodeStateAsUpdate(a);
  const b = new Y.Doc(); Y.applyUpdate(b, update); Y.applyUpdate(b, update);
  expect(readColumns(b)).toHaveLength(1);
});

      Stretch: an integration test that starts the Hocuspocus server on a random port inside Vitest (Node environment) and connects two providers, asserting that a change on one arrives on the other.

      Interview questions this milestone prepares

      WebSockets vs Server-Sent Events vs polling for this app?

      Collaboration needs low-latency two-way messages (edits and presence both directions), so WebSockets. SSE is one-way server→client; polling adds latency and load. See Real-time communication.

      What happens when a user edits offline for an hour and reconnects?

      Edits are applied locally and stored in IndexedDB. On reconnect, client and server exchange state vectors, send only the missing updates, and the CRDT merges them; both sides converge without manual conflict resolution.

      What is a state vector?

      A compact summary of how many operations from each client a replica has seen. Comparing state vectors tells you exactly which updates the other side is missing.

      Done when

      Two browser windows edit the same board in real time; switching one to offline in DevTools shows the offline message, edits keep working, and going back online syncs both ways automatically.

      C5 · Conflicts and data integrity

      Build

      • Understand and document what happens for every kind of concurrent edit on your board.
      • A deterministic repairBoard() that fixes the situations CRDTs can't prevent: orphaned cards and duplicate order keys.
      • A message when a card you're viewing is deleted by someone else.
      • A schema version and a migration path for future changes to the document shape.
      • Concurrency tests, including a randomised "fuzz" test with three devices.

      Concepts: why and syntax

      What the CRDT guarantees, and what it doesn't

      Yjs guarantees convergence: every device ends with the same document. It doesn't guarantee your application's rules (every card belongs to an existing column, orders are unique). Those are your job.

      Concurrent situationWhat Yjs doesWhat you do
      Alice renames a card, Bob moves itBoth apply (different fields)Nothing: this is why each card is a Y.Map
      Alice and Bob rename the same cardOne value wins, the same on every deviceAcceptable for short fields (last-writer-wins)
      Both edit the description textCharacter-level merge; both edits keptNothing (Y.XmlFragment / Y.Text)
      Both move the same card to different placesOne columnId/order wins; the card exists onceNothing: no duplicates thanks to field-based moves
      Both append a card at the end of a columnBoth kept, but identical order keys (same inputs → same key)Tie-break by id when sorting; repair re-spaces keys
      Alice deletes a column, Bob adds a card to itColumn gone, Bob's card kept with a dangling columnIdRepair moves orphans to the first column
      Alice deletes a card Bob is editingDelete wins; Bob's later edits go into a removed mapDetect it and tell Bob; offer "Restore" from his copy

      A deterministic repair pass

      ts
      // packages/shared/src/repair.ts
export const REPAIR_ORIGIN = "repair";                 // not tracked by undo (C8)

export function repairBoard(doc: Y.Doc): boolean {
  const { columns, cards } = getBoard(doc);
  const cols = readColumns(doc);
  if (cols.length === 0) return false;
  let changed = false;

  doc.transact(() => {
    // 1. Orphaned cards → first column
    for (const card of cards.values()) {
      if (!columns.has(card.get("columnId") as string)) {
        card.set("columnId", cols[0].id);
        changed = true;
      }
    }
    // 2. Duplicate or out-of-order keys → re-space, keeping the current (id tie-broken) order
    for (const col of cols) {
      const list = readCards(doc, col.id);
      let prev: string | null = null;
      for (let i = 0; i < list.length; i++) {
        if (prev !== null && list[i].order <= prev) {
          const next = list.slice(i + 1).find(c => c.order > prev!)?.order ?? null;
          const key = generateKeyBetween(prev, next);
          cards.get(list[i].id)!.set("order", key);
          prev = key;
          changed = true;
        } else {
          prev = list[i].order;
        }
      }
    }
  }, REPAIR_ORIGIN);
  return changed;
}

      Why "deterministic" matters: two devices may run repair at the same moment. Because the same input always produces the same keys and columns, both write identical values and still converge. Never use Math.random() or timestamps in repair logic.

      ts
      // run after the first sync and after remote changes (debounced), only for users who can edit
const scheduleRepair = debounce(() => repairBoard(doc), 500);
doc.on("update", (_update: Uint8Array, origin: unknown) => {
  if (origin !== REPAIR_ORIGIN) scheduleRepair();
});

      Detecting "deleted while you were editing"

      ts
      useEffect(() => {
  const { cards } = getBoard(doc);
  const onChange = (event: Y.YMapEvent<Y.Map<unknown>>) => {
    if (event.changes.keys.get(openCardId)?.action === "delete" && !event.transaction.local) {
      setNotice("This card was deleted by someone else. You can restore it from your copy.");
    }
  };
  cards.observe(onChange);
  return () => cards.unobserve(onChange);
}, [doc, openCardId]);

      Schema versioning

      ts
      export const SCHEMA_VERSION = 2;
export function migrate(doc: Y.Doc) {
  const { meta, cards } = getBoard(doc);
  const version = (meta.get("schemaVersion") as number | undefined) ?? 1;
  if (version >= SCHEMA_VERSION) return;
  doc.transact(() => {
    if (version < 2) for (const card of cards.values()) if (!card.has("labels")) card.set("labels", new Y.Array());
    meta.set("schemaVersion", SCHEMA_VERSION);
  }, REPAIR_ORIGIN);
}

      Old app versions may still be open in other tabs after a deploy; migrations must be additive (add fields with defaults, never repurpose them) so old clients don't break the document.

      Tests

      ts
      function twoDevices() {
  const a = new Y.Doc(), b = new Y.Doc();
  const todo = addColumn(a, "To do"), done = addColumn(a, "Done");
  const card = addCard(a, { columnId: todo, title: "Task", userId: "a" });
  syncDocs(a, b);
  return { a, b, todo, done, card };
}
// Compare documents with sorted keys: map iteration order can differ between replicas even when contents match
const stable = (v: unknown): unknown =>
  Array.isArray(v) ? v.map(stable)
  : v && typeof v === "object" ? Object.fromEntries(Object.keys(v).sort().map(k => [k, stable((v as Record<string, unknown>)[k])]))
  : v;
const snapshot = (d: Y.Doc) =>
  JSON.stringify(stable({ columns: getBoard(d).columns.toJSON(), cards: getBoard(d).cards.toJSON() }));

it("rename and move of the same card both survive", () => {
  const { a, b, done, card } = twoDevices();
  renameCard(a, card, "Renamed");           // offline on A
  moveCard(b, card, done, 0);               // offline on B
  syncDocs(a, b);
  const c = getBoard(a).cards.get(card)!.toJSON();
  expect(c).toMatchObject({ title: "Renamed", columnId: done });
  expect(snapshot(a)).toBe(snapshot(b));
});
it("concurrent moves of the same card: it exists exactly once, same place everywhere", () => {
  const { a, b, todo, done, card } = twoDevices();
  moveCard(a, card, done, 0);
  moveCard(b, card, todo, 0);
  syncDocs(a, b);
  const all = [...readCards(a, todo), ...readCards(a, done)].filter(c => c.id === card);
  expect(all).toHaveLength(1);
  expect(snapshot(a)).toBe(snapshot(b));
});
it("delete column vs add card: repair rescues the orphan", () => {
  const { a, b, todo, done } = twoDevices();
  deleteColumn(a, done);
  const orphan = addCard(b, { columnId: done, title: "Late", userId: "b" });
  syncDocs(a, b);
  repairBoard(a); repairBoard(b); syncDocs(a, b);
  expect(readCards(a, todo).map(c => c.id)).toContain(orphan);
  expect(snapshot(a)).toBe(snapshot(b));
});
it("concurrent appends get unique keys after repair", () => {
  const { a, b, todo } = twoDevices();
  addCard(a, { columnId: todo, title: "A2", userId: "a" });
  addCard(b, { columnId: todo, title: "B2", userId: "b" });
  syncDocs(a, b);
  repairBoard(a); repairBoard(b); syncDocs(a, b);
  const orders = readCards(a, todo).map(c => c.order);
  expect(new Set(orders).size).toBe(orders.length);
});

      Fuzz test: random operations on three devices must always converge

      ts
      it("converges under random concurrent operations", () => {
  let seed = 42;
  const rand = () => (seed = (seed * 16807) % 2147483647) / 2147483647;   // deterministic pseudo-random
  const docs = [new Y.Doc(), new Y.Doc(), new Y.Doc()];
  const cols = [addColumn(docs[0], "A"), addColumn(docs[0], "B"), addColumn(docs[0], "C")];
  docs.forEach(d => syncDocs(docs[0], d));

  for (let step = 0; step < 500; step++) {
    const doc = docs[Math.floor(rand() * 3)];
    const ids = [...getBoard(doc).cards.keys()];
    const op = rand();
    if (op < 0.4 || ids.length === 0) addCard(doc, { columnId: cols[Math.floor(rand() * 3)], title: `c${step}`, userId: "u" });
    else if (op < 0.75) moveCard(doc, ids[Math.floor(rand() * ids.length)], cols[Math.floor(rand() * 3)], Math.floor(rand() * 5));
    else if (op < 0.9) renameCard(doc, ids[Math.floor(rand() * ids.length)], `r${step}`);
    else getBoard(doc).cards.delete(ids[Math.floor(rand() * ids.length)]);
    if (rand() < 0.2) syncDocs(docs[Math.floor(rand() * 3)], docs[Math.floor(rand() * 3)]);   // random partial syncs
  }
  for (let i = 0; i < 2; i++) { syncDocs(docs[0], docs[1]); syncDocs(docs[1], docs[2]); syncDocs(docs[0], docs[2]); }
  docs.forEach(repairBoard);
  for (let i = 0; i < 2; i++) { syncDocs(docs[0], docs[1]); syncDocs(docs[1], docs[2]); syncDocs(docs[0], docs[2]); }

  expect(snapshot(docs[1])).toBe(snapshot(docs[0]));
  expect(snapshot(docs[2])).toBe(snapshot(docs[0]));
  for (const col of cols) {                                           // invariants hold
    const orders = readCards(docs[0], col).map(c => c.order);
    expect(new Set(orders).size).toBe(orders.length);
  }
});

      For a stronger version, use the fast-check library (property-based testing): it generates operation sequences and shrinks failures to the smallest reproducing case.

      Interview questions this milestone prepares

      CRDT vs Operational Transformation (OT)?

      OT (Google Docs) transforms operations against each other through a central server that orders them. CRDTs (Yjs, Automerge) design data so merges commute, working peer-to-peer and offline without a central authority. CRDTs are simpler to run offline; OT is mature for centralised text editing.

      If the CRDT converges, why do you need a repair step?

      Convergence means everyone has the same state, not a valid one. Application invariants (no orphaned cards, unique order keys) can be broken by concurrent edits, so a deterministic repair restores them.

      How do you test conflict handling without flaky timing?

      Simulate devices as separate Y.Docs, apply operations "offline", then exchange updates explicitly. Deterministic, fast, no network. Add a seeded fuzz test for random interleavings, and a few E2E tests for the real transport.

      How do you evolve the document schema after release?

      Store a schema version, run additive, deterministic migrations on load, never repurpose fields, and keep old clients working since they may still be connected.

      Done when

      Every row of the conflict table has a test, the fuzz test passes with several seeds, and deletions of open cards are handled in the UI.

      C6 · Presence: cursors, avatars and activity

      Build

      • Avatars of everyone currently on the board.
      • Live cursors with names, smoothly updated.
      • Indicators on cards: "Priya is dragging this" / "Aman is editing".

      Concepts: why and syntax

      Awareness: ephemeral state, separate from the document

      Why not store cursors in the Y.Doc? Cursor positions change 20 times a second and are worthless a minute later. Putting them in the document would bloat history forever. Yjs's awareness protocol shares small, temporary per-user state that disappears when the user leaves (or after ~30 s without updates).

      ts
      // after the provider connects (C4)
const awareness = provider.awareness!;
awareness.setLocalStateField("user", { id: me.id, name: me.name, color: colorFor(me.id) });

// cursor in board-content coordinates, so different window sizes and scroll positions line up
const onPointerMove = throttle((e: PointerEvent) => {
  const el = boardRef.current!;
  const rect = el.getBoundingClientRect();
  awareness.setLocalStateField("cursor", {
    x: e.clientX - rect.left + el.scrollLeft,
    y: e.clientY - rect.top + el.scrollTop,
  });
}, 40);                                                   // ~25 updates/s is plenty
const onPointerLeave = () => awareness.setLocalStateField("cursor", null);

// activity
awareness.setLocalStateField("activity", { type: "dragging", cardId });   // on drag start
awareness.setLocalStateField("activity", null);                           // on drag end
      ts
      // usePeers.ts
export type Peer = { clientId: number; user: { id: string; name: string; color: string };
                     cursor?: { x: number; y: number } | null; activity?: { type: string; cardId: string } | null };

export function usePeers(awareness: Awareness | undefined): Peer[] {
  const [peers, setPeers] = useState<Peer[]>([]);
  useEffect(() => {
    if (!awareness) return;
    const update = () => setPeers(
      [...awareness.getStates()]
        .filter(([clientId, state]) => clientId !== awareness.clientID && state.user)
        .map(([clientId, state]) => ({ clientId, ...state }) as Peer)
    );
    update();
    awareness.on("change", update);
    return () => awareness.off("change", update);
  }, [awareness]);
  return peers;
}
      tsx
      // Cursors.tsx — positioned inside the scrolling board container
{peers.filter(p => p.cursor).map(p => (
  <div key={p.clientId} className="cursor" aria-hidden="true"
       style={{ transform: `translate(${p.cursor!.x}px, ${p.cursor!.y}px)`, color: p.user.color }}>
    <svg width="16" height="16" viewBox="0 0 16 16"><path d="M0 0l6 15 2-6 6-2z" fill="currentColor" /></svg>
    <span style={{ background: p.user.color }}>{p.user.name}</span>
  </div>
))}
      css
      .cursor { position: absolute; top: 0; left: 0; pointer-events: none; transition: transform 80ms linear; }
@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { .cursor { transition: none; } }
      • One user with two tabs appears twice (two client ids). Group avatars by user.id, cursors by client.
      • Pick readable colours with enough contrast for name labels; derive them from the user id so they're stable.
      • Cursors are decorative for screen readers (aria-hidden); announce joins/leaves politely instead ("Priya joined the board").
      • Clear local presence on unmount/tab hide (awareness.setLocalState(null)) so ghosts don't linger.

      Tests

      Awareness is easiest to test end to end (C11: two browser contexts, one sees the other's avatar and cursor). Unit-test the helpers: colorFor(id) is stable and readable, peers are grouped by user, the throttle limits updates (fake timers).

      Interview questions this milestone prepares

      Why keep presence out of the document?

      It's high-frequency, short-lived and per-connection. Storing it in the CRDT would grow the document history forever and sync useless data to future readers. Awareness is ephemeral and garbage-collected on disconnect.

      How do you keep cursor updates from overwhelming the network and React?

      Throttle sends (~25/s), send positions in content coordinates, render cursors with CSS transforms (compositor-only), and isolate them in a small component so the board itself doesn't re-render.

      Done when

      With two windows side by side, each sees the other's avatar, labelled cursor and "dragging" indicator, and they disappear within seconds after closing a window.

      C7 · Collaborative rich-text card descriptions

      Build

      • A card detail dialog with a rich-text description (headings, lists, checklists, links) that several people can edit at the same time, with their carets visible.

      Concepts: why and syntax

      Why a CRDT text type: a plain string field is last-writer-wins, so two people typing at once would overwrite each other. Rich text stored as a Y.XmlFragment merges at the character level, and editors like Tiptap (built on ProseMirror) bind to it directly.

      bash
      pnpm --filter web add @tiptap/react @tiptap/starter-kit @tiptap/extension-collaboration @tiptap/extension-collaboration-caret
# Tiptap 2 names the caret extension @tiptap/extension-collaboration-cursor — use the one matching your Tiptap version
      tsx
      // CardDescription.tsx
export function CardDescription({ card, provider, me }: { card: Y.Map<unknown>; provider: HocuspocusProvider; me: User }) {
  const fragment = card.get("description") as Y.XmlFragment;     // created in addCard (C1)
  const editor = useEditor({
    extensions: [
      StarterKit.configure({ undoRedo: false }),   // Tiptap 3 (Tiptap 2: history: false) — Yjs provides collaborative undo
      Collaboration.configure({ fragment }),
      CollaborationCaret.configure({ provider, user: { name: me.name, color: colorFor(me.id) } }),
    ],
    editable: canEdit,
  }, [fragment]);
  return (
    <div>
      <label id="desc-label">Description</label>
      <EditorContent editor={editor} aria-labelledby="desc-label" />
    </div>
  );
}
      • Built-in editor undo must be turned off: two undo systems would fight. Collaborative undo only undoes your own typing.
      • Render descriptions read-only for viewers (editable: false); the server also enforces it (C9).
      • Sanitise any HTML you export (for example, email notifications) exactly as you would user input.

      Tests

      ts
      it("concurrent typing in the same description keeps both edits", () => {
  const { a, b, card } = twoDevices();
  const fa = getBoard(a).cards.get(card)!.get("description") as Y.XmlFragment;
  const fb = getBoard(b).cards.get(card)!.get("description") as Y.XmlFragment;
  const para = (text: string) => { const p = new Y.XmlElement("paragraph"); p.insert(0, [new Y.XmlText(text)]); return p; };
  fa.insert(0, [para("from A")]);
  fb.insert(0, [para("from B")]);
  syncDocs(a, b);
  expect(fa.toString()).toBe(fb.toString());
  expect(fa.toString()).toContain("from A");
  expect(fa.toString()).toContain("from B");
});

      Plus E2E (C11): two users type in the same description and both see the combined text.

      Interview questions this milestone prepares

      How do collaborative editors avoid overwriting each other's text?

      Each character (or element) has a unique id and a position relative to its neighbours, so concurrent inserts are ordered deterministically and deletes only remove specific ids. Every replica ends with the same text containing both users' edits.

      Done when

      Two users can type in the same description simultaneously, see each other's carets, and never lose text.

      C8 · Undo and redo

      Build

      • Undo/redo buttons and shortcuts (Ctrl/Cmd+Z, Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+Z, Ctrl+Y) for board actions.
      • Undo only reverts your changes, never a collaborator's, and never repair or migration writes.

      Concepts: why and syntax

      Why origins matter: in a single-user app, undo reverses the last change. In a collaborative app the last change might be someone else's; undoing it would silently delete their work. Yjs's UndoManager tracks only transactions whose origin you list.

      ts
      // apps/web/src/sync/useUndo.ts
export function useUndo(doc: Y.Doc) {
  const [manager, setManager] = useState<Y.UndoManager | null>(null);
  const [canUndo, setCanUndo] = useState(false);
  const [canRedo, setCanRedo] = useState(false);

  // Create inside an effect (not useMemo): Strict Mode runs cleanup + setup again in development,
  // and a memoised manager would be used after it was destroyed.
  useEffect(() => {
    const { columns, cards } = getBoard(doc);
    const m = new Y.UndoManager([columns, cards], {
      trackedOrigins: new Set([LOCAL_ORIGIN]),   // only transactions tagged by board ops (C1)
      captureTimeout: 400,                       // edits within 400 ms merge into one undo step
    });
    const refresh = () => { setCanUndo(m.undoStack.length > 0); setCanRedo(m.redoStack.length > 0); };
    m.on("stack-item-added", refresh);
    m.on("stack-item-popped", refresh);
    setManager(m);
    return () => m.destroy();
  }, [doc]);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (!manager) return;
    const onKey = (e: KeyboardEvent) => {
      if ((e.target as HTMLElement).closest("[contenteditable], input, textarea")) return;  // editors handle their own undo
      const mod = e.metaKey || e.ctrlKey;
      const key = e.key.toLowerCase();
      if (mod && key === "z" && !e.shiftKey) { e.preventDefault(); manager.undo(); }
      else if (mod && (key === "y" || (key === "z" && e.shiftKey))) { e.preventDefault(); manager.redo(); }
    };
    window.addEventListener("keydown", onKey);
    return () => window.removeEventListener("keydown", onKey);
  }, [manager]);

  return { undo: () => manager?.undo(), redo: () => manager?.redo(), canUndo, canRedo };
}

      Tests

      ts
      it("undo reverts my move but not a collaborator's rename", () => {
  const { a, b, done, card } = twoDevices();
  const undo = new Y.UndoManager([getBoard(a).cards], { trackedOrigins: new Set([LOCAL_ORIGIN]) });
  moveCard(a, card, done, 0);                     // my change
  renameCard(b, card, "Bob's title");             // collaborator
  syncDocs(a, b);
  undo.undo();
  const c = getBoard(a).cards.get(card)!.toJSON();
  expect(c.columnId).not.toBe(done);              // my move reverted
  expect(c.title).toBe("Bob's title");            // their rename kept
});
it("repair writes are not undoable", () => { /* run repairBoard, expect undoStack length 0 */ });

      Interview questions this milestone prepares

      How is undo different in a collaborative app?

      Undo must be per-user and selective: revert only your own operations, applied on top of everyone's current state, without touching others' changes. Tag transactions with an origin and track only yours.

      Done when

      Undo/redo works with buttons and shortcuts, and a test proves it never reverts a collaborator's change.

      C9 · Accounts, roles and invite links

      Build

      • Sign up / log in with email and password; the session is an HttpOnly cookie.
      • Boards have members with roles: owner (everything, manage members), editor (edit), viewer (read only).
      • Invite links with a role and expiry; owners can change roles and remove members.
      • The sync server enforces roles: non-members can't connect, viewers' edits are rejected.

      Concepts: why and syntax

      SQL schema (Schema design)

      sql
      -- apps/server/src/schema.sql  (SQLite via better-sqlite3; run once at startup)
PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON;
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS users (
  id            TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  email         TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE COLLATE NOCASE,
  name          TEXT NOT NULL,
  password_hash TEXT NOT NULL,
  created_at    INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT (unixepoch())
);
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS boards (
  id         TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  title      TEXT NOT NULL,
  owner_id   TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  created_at INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT (unixepoch())
);
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS board_members (
  board_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES boards(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  user_id  TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  role     TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (role IN ('owner', 'editor', 'viewer')),
  PRIMARY KEY (board_id, user_id)
);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_members_user ON board_members (user_id);
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS invites (
  token      TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  board_id   TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES boards(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  role       TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (role IN ('editor', 'viewer')),
  created_by TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id),
  expires_at INTEGER NOT NULL
);
      ts
      // apps/server/src/db.ts
import Database from "better-sqlite3";
export const db = new Database(process.env.DB_PATH ?? "data/app.sqlite");
db.exec(readFileSync(new URL("./schema.sql", import.meta.url), "utf8"));

export type Role = "owner" | "editor" | "viewer";
export const getRole = (boardId: string, userId: string) =>
  (db.prepare("SELECT role FROM board_members WHERE board_id = ? AND user_id = ?").get(boardId, userId) as
    { role: Role } | undefined)?.role ?? null;

export const listBoards = (userId: string) =>
  db.prepare(`SELECT b.id, b.title, m.role FROM boards b
              JOIN board_members m ON m.board_id = b.id
              WHERE m.user_id = ? ORDER BY b.created_at DESC`).all(userId);

export const acceptInvite = db.transaction((token: string, userId: string) => {   // all-or-nothing
  const invite = db.prepare("SELECT board_id, role FROM invites WHERE token = ? AND expires_at > unixepoch()")
                   .get(token) as { board_id: string; role: Role } | undefined;
  if (!invite) return null;
  db.prepare(`INSERT INTO board_members (board_id, user_id, role) VALUES (?, ?, ?)
              ON CONFLICT (board_id, user_id) DO NOTHING`)        // never downgrade an existing owner
    .run(invite.board_id, userId, invite.role);
  return invite.board_id;
});

      Every query uses ? placeholders: parameterised, so no SQL injection (SQL in app code).

      Sessions and a short-lived WebSocket token (Web authentication)

      Why two tokens: the session lives in an HttpOnly cookie (JavaScript can't read it, so XSS can't steal it). The WebSocket connects to a different server, so the client asks the API for a 60-second token just for the handshake. If it leaks, it's useless a minute later.

      bash
      pnpm --filter server add hono @hono/node-server better-sqlite3 jose bcryptjs zod
pnpm --filter server add -D @types/better-sqlite3 @types/bcryptjs
      ts
      // apps/server/src/auth.ts
import { SignJWT, jwtVerify } from "jose";
const secret = new TextEncoder().encode(process.env.JWT_SECRET!);          // long random string, from env

export const signToken = (user: { id: string; name: string }, expiresIn: string) =>
  new SignJWT({ name: user.name }).setProtectedHeader({ alg: "HS256" })
    .setSubject(user.id).setIssuedAt().setExpirationTime(expiresIn).sign(secret);

export async function verifyToken(token: string) {
  const { payload } = await jwtVerify(token, secret);                       // throws if invalid or expired
  return { id: payload.sub!, name: String(payload.name) };
}
      ts
      // apps/server/src/api.ts (key routes)
const app = new Hono<{ Variables: { user: { id: string; name: string } } }>();
const Credentials = z.object({ email: z.email(), password: z.string().min(10), name: z.string().min(1).optional() });

app.post("/api/auth/signup", async c => {
  const body = Credentials.safeParse(await c.req.json());
  if (!body.success || !body.data.name) return c.json({ error: "Enter your name, a valid email and a password of 10+ characters" }, 422);
  const id = crypto.randomUUID();
  try {
    db.prepare("INSERT INTO users (id, email, name, password_hash) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)")
      .run(id, body.data.email, body.data.name, await bcrypt.hash(body.data.password, 12));
  } catch { return c.json({ error: "An account with this email already exists" }, 409); }
  await startSession(c, { id, name: body.data.name });
  return c.json({ id, name: body.data.name }, 201);
});

async function startSession(c: Context, user: { id: string; name: string }) {
  setCookie(c, "session", await signToken(user, "7d"), {
    httpOnly: true, secure: process.env.NODE_ENV === "production", sameSite: "Lax", path: "/", maxAge: 60 * 60 * 24 * 7,
  });
}

const requireUser = createMiddleware(async (c, next) => {
  try { c.set("user", await verifyToken(getCookie(c, "session") ?? "")); }
  catch { return c.json({ error: "Please log in" }, 401); }
  await next();
});

app.get("/api/auth/ws-token", requireUser, async c => c.json({ token: await signToken(c.get("user"), "60s") }));
app.get("/api/boards", requireUser, c => c.json(listBoards(c.get("user").id)));
app.post("/api/boards/:id/invites", requireUser, async c => {
  if (getRole(c.req.param("id"), c.get("user").id) !== "owner") return c.json({ error: "Only owners can invite" }, 403);
  // insert into invites with a random token (crypto.randomUUID()) and expires_at = now + 7 days; return the URL
});
serve({ fetch: app.fetch, port: 3001 });

      In development, proxy /api from Vite to port 3001 (server.proxy) so cookies are same-origin. In production, serve the API under the same site (or configure CORS with credentials; see CORS). For a real product, consider Better Auth or Supabase Auth instead of hand-rolled auth; building it once teaches you what those libraries do.

      Enforcing roles on the sync server

      ts
      // apps/server/src/sync.ts
export async function authenticate(token: string, documentName: string) {  // pure, easy to unit-test
  const user = await verifyToken(token);                                    // throws → connection rejected
  const boardId = documentName.replace(/^board:/, "");
  const role = getRole(boardId, user.id);
  if (!role) throw new Error("Not a member of this board");
  return { user, role };
}

new Server({
  // ...
  async onAuthenticate({ token, documentName, connectionConfig }) {   // Hocuspocus v2: data.connection.readOnly
    const { user, role } = await authenticate(token, documentName);
    if (role === "viewer") connectionConfig.readOnly = true;          // server drops their document updates
    return { user, role };                                            // available as context in other hooks
  },
});
      • The client hides edit controls for viewers (disable drag sensors, read-only editor, no add buttons, a "View only" banner), but the server is what enforces it.
      • When an owner removes someone or downgrades them, close that user's connections on the sync server so they must re-authenticate with their new role.
      • Offline edge case: a removed user may have made offline edits. On reconnect, authentication fails; show "You no longer have access to this board", stop syncing, and clear the local copy. Explain this trade-off in your README.

      Tests

      ts
      // Hono apps can be tested without starting a server: app.request()
beforeEach(() => resetDatabase(":memory:"));

it("rejects unauthenticated requests", async () => {
  const res = await app.request("/api/boards");
  expect(res.status).toBe(401);
});
it("only owners can create invites", async () => {
  const { cookie: editorCookie } = await signUpAndJoin("editor");
  const res = await app.request(`/api/boards/${boardId}/invites`, { method: "POST", headers: { cookie: editorCookie } });
  expect(res.status).toBe(403);
});
it("expired invites are refused", () => {
  insertInvite({ token: "t1", boardId, role: "editor", expiresAt: Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000) - 1 });
  expect(acceptInvite("t1", userId)).toBeNull();
});
it("the sync server refuses non-members", async () => {
  const token = await signToken({ id: "stranger", name: "S" }, "60s");
  await expect(authenticate(token, `board:${boardId}`)).rejects.toThrow(/not a member/i);
});
it("viewers get read-only connections", async () => { /* authenticate returns role "viewer" */ });

      Interview questions this milestone prepares

      How do you authenticate a WebSocket connection?

      Browsers can't set custom headers on WebSocket handshakes, so send a short-lived token in the first message or the URL, verify it on the server before joining the document, and check authorisation (membership, role) at the same time. Cookies also work when the socket server is on the same site.

      Where do you enforce permissions?

      On the server, always: REST routes check the role, the sync server rejects non-members and makes viewer connections read-only. The client only hides controls for a better experience.

      Why store the session in an HttpOnly cookie?

      JavaScript can't read it, so an XSS bug can't exfiltrate it. Pair it with Secure and SameSite=Lax to limit CSRF.

      Done when

      Invites work end to end, viewers can't change anything (even by calling the socket directly), non-members can't connect, and API and authentication tests pass.

      C10 · PWA: install, offline app shell, safe updates

      Build

      • SyncBoard installs as an app (desktop and Android; "Add to Home Screen" on iOS).
      • After the first visit, the app itself loads with no network (data already works offline since C3).
      • When a new version is deployed, users see "A new version is available · Reload" instead of being switched mid-edit.

      Concepts: why and syntax

      Why a service worker: IndexedDB keeps your data offline, but the HTML, JS and CSS still come from the network. A service worker caches the app shell so it opens instantly and offline. See PWAs.

      bash
      pnpm --filter web add -D vite-plugin-pwa
      ts
      // apps/web/vite.config.ts
import { VitePWA } from "vite-plugin-pwa";
plugins: [
  react(),
  VitePWA({
    registerType: "prompt",                         // don't swap versions under the user's feet
    manifest: {
      name: "SyncBoard", short_name: "SyncBoard", start_url: "/", display: "standalone",
      theme_color: "#4f46e5", background_color: "#ffffff",
      icons: [
        { src: "/pwa-192.png", sizes: "192x192", type: "image/png" },
        { src: "/pwa-512.png", sizes: "512x512", type: "image/png" },
        { src: "/pwa-512-maskable.png", sizes: "512x512", type: "image/png", purpose: "maskable" },
      ],
    },
    workbox: {
      globPatterns: ["**/*.{js,css,html,svg,png,woff2}"],   // precache the built app shell
      navigateFallback: "/index.html",                        // any route works offline (SPA)
      navigateFallbackDenylist: [/^\/api\//],                 // never serve the shell for API calls
    },
  }),
],
      tsx
      // UpdatePrompt.tsx
import { useRegisterSW } from "virtual:pwa-register/react";
export function UpdatePrompt() {
  const { needRefresh: [needRefresh, setNeedRefresh], updateServiceWorker } = useRegisterSW();
  if (!needRefresh) return null;
  return (
    <div role="status" className="toast">
      A new version of SyncBoard is available.
      <button onClick={() => updateServiceWorker(true)}>Reload</button>
      <button onClick={() => setNeedRefresh(false)}>Later</button>
    </div>
  );
}
      ts
      // Install button (Chromium browsers fire beforeinstallprompt; Safari uses Share → Add to Home Screen)
let deferred: any = null;
window.addEventListener("beforeinstallprompt", e => { e.preventDefault(); deferred = e; showInstallButton(); });
async function install() { deferred?.prompt(); await deferred?.userChoice; deferred = null; hideInstallButton(); }

      Service worker lifecycle in one paragraph

      A new service worker installs in the background (precaching the new files), then waits while old tabs still use the old version; it activates when all old tabs close or when you tell it to skip waiting (the Reload button). This is why "prompt" is safer than auto-update for an editing app: activating mid-session could load new JS against an old in-memory state.

      Tests

      • Manual: DevTools → Application → Manifest (installability) and Service Workers; Network → Offline, reload: the app and your boards still load.
      • E2E (C11): build, run vite preview, visit once, go offline, reload, assert the board renders.

      Interview questions this milestone prepares

      What makes a web app installable?

      HTTPS, a web app manifest (name, icons, start URL, display mode), and in most browsers a registered service worker. Browsers then offer installation.

      Why is updating a PWA tricky?

      The new service worker waits until old clients are gone. Forcing activation mid-session can mix old and new code or cached assets. Prompt the user, then reload cleanly.

      Done when

      The app installs, launches offline after one visit, and shows the update prompt after you redeploy a change.

      C11 · E2E with two browsers, CI and deployment

      Build

      • Playwright tests where two independent users (separate browser contexts) edit the same board at once, including one going offline.
      • A CI pipeline that starts both servers and runs everything.
      • Deployment with a WebSocket-capable host, TLS, persistent storage and backups.

      Concepts: why and syntax

      Two contexts = two users

      A Playwright browser context is like a separate incognito profile: its own cookies, storage and IndexedDB. Two contexts in one test give you two real users talking through your real sync server.

      ts
      // playwright.config.ts
export default defineConfig({
  testDir: "e2e",
  use: { baseURL: "http://localhost:4173", trace: "on-first-retry" },
  webServer: [
    { command: "pnpm --filter server start:test", url: "http://localhost:3001/health", reuseExistingServer: !process.env.CI },
    { command: "pnpm --filter web build && pnpm --filter web preview --port 4173", url: "http://localhost:4173", reuseExistingServer: !process.env.CI },
  ],
});
      ts
      // e2e/collaboration.spec.ts
import { test, expect, type Browser } from "@playwright/test";

async function twoUsersOnOneBoard(browser: Browser) {
  const aliceCtx = await browser.newContext(), bobCtx = await browser.newContext();
  const alice = await aliceCtx.newPage(), bob = await bobCtx.newPage();
  const boardUrl = await signUpAndCreateBoard(alice, "Alice");        // helpers using the UI or the API
  const inviteUrl = await createInvite(alice, "editor");
  await signUpAndAcceptInvite(bob, "Bob", inviteUrl);
  await bob.goto(boardUrl);
  return { alice, bob, aliceCtx, bobCtx };
}

test("edits appear for the other user in real time, with presence", async ({ browser }) => {
  const { alice, bob } = await twoUsersOnOneBoard(browser);
  await alice.getByRole("button", { name: "Add card to To do" }).click();
  await alice.getByRole("textbox", { name: "Card title" }).fill("Write E2E tests");
  await alice.keyboard.press("Enter");

  await expect(bob.getByRole("region", { name: /to do/i })).toContainText("Write E2E tests");
  await expect(bob.getByRole("list", { name: "People on this board" })).toContainText("Alice");
});

test("offline edits on both sides merge after reconnecting", async ({ browser }) => {
  const { alice, bob, bobCtx } = await twoUsersOnOneBoard(browser);
  await addCard(alice, "To do", "Shared card");
  await expect(bob.getByText("Shared card")).toBeVisible();

  await bobCtx.setOffline(true);                                      // fires "offline" → provider disconnects (C4)
  await expect(bob.getByRole("status")).toContainText(/offline/i);
  await renameCard(bob, "Shared card", "Renamed by Bob offline");
  await moveCardWithKeyboard(alice, "Shared card", "Done");           // concurrent change on a different field

  await bobCtx.setOffline(false);
  for (const page of [alice, bob]) {
    const done = page.getByRole("region", { name: /done/i });
    await expect(done).toContainText("Renamed by Bob offline");       // both edits survived, same result on both
  }
  await expect(bob.getByRole("status")).toContainText(/all changes synced/i);
});

test("keyboard drag and drop moves a card", async ({ browser }) => {
  const { alice } = await twoUsersOnOneBoard(browser);
  await addCard(alice, "To do", "Keyboard card");
  const card = alice.getByRole("button", { name: /keyboard card/i });  // dnd-kit makes sortable items focusable
  await card.focus();
  await alice.keyboard.press("Space");
  await alice.keyboard.press("ArrowRight");
  await alice.keyboard.press("Space");
  await expect(alice.getByRole("region", { name: /doing/i })).toContainText("Keyboard card");
});

test("viewers cannot edit", async ({ browser }) => { /* invite as viewer: no add buttons, dragging does nothing, banner shown */ });
test("the app loads offline after the first visit", async ({ browser }) => {
  const ctx = await browser.newContext();
  const page = await ctx.newPage();
  const boardUrl = await signUpAndCreateBoard(page, "Solo");
  await page.waitForFunction(() => navigator.serviceWorker.controller !== null);   // SW is controlling the page
  await ctx.setOffline(true);
  await page.goto(boardUrl);
  await expect(page.getByRole("region", { name: /to do/i })).toBeVisible();
});

      Why these are deterministic: the app reacts to the offline/online events that setOffline fires, and every assertion waits for an outcome (toContainText retries) instead of sleeping. Use a separate test database (start:test with DB_PATH=:memory: or a temp file) so runs are isolated.

      CI

      yaml
      # .github/workflows/ci.yml
name: CI
on: [push, pull_request]
jobs:
  test:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest

      - run: pnpm -r lint && pnpm -r typecheck

      - run: pnpm exec playwright test
      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        if: failure()

      Deployment

      PieceWhereNotes
      Web app (static + service worker)Vercel, Netlify or Cloudflare PagesSPA fallback to index.html; HTTPS is automatic
      API + sync server (Node, long-lived WebSockets)Fly.io, Railway or RenderNot serverless functions: WebSockets need a long-running process. Use wss://
      SQLite filesA persistent volume on that hostNightly backups (Litestream or a cron copy to object storage)
      SecretsHost's environment settingsJWT_SECRET, never committed

      Scaling beyond one server: clients editing the same document must see each other's updates even when connected to different instances. Hocuspocus has a Redis extension that relays updates between instances; persistence can move to Postgres with the database extension. Mention this in interviews even if you run a single instance.

      Done when

      All E2E tests pass locally and in CI, the app is live on HTTPS/WSS, two phones on different networks can edit the same board, and the README documents the architecture, conflict rules and trade-offs.

      Presenting SyncBoard, and the interview questions it answers

      The 2-minute project walkthrough

      1. What: "A Trello-style board that works fully offline and syncs in real time, with presence, undo, permissions, and installable as an app."
